to trigger this problem.)\n\nWhen `x.example.com` responds with `Strict-Transport-Security:` headers, this\nbug can make the subdomain's expiry timeout *bleed over* and get set for the\nparent domain `example.com` in curl's HSTS cache.\n\nThe result of a triggered bug is that HTTP accesses to `example.com` get\nconverted to HTTPS for a different period of time than what was asked for by\nthe origin server. The re_dfa_add_node (the NFA node table) and build_range_exp (bracket ranges) grow several arrays with consecutive realloc calls and store the new pointers only after all of them succeed.  When a later call fails, the pattern buffer keeps pointers to blocks that were already freed, and the error cleanup frees them again.  In regexec, the back-reference cache (match_ctx_add_entry) frees its array when a realloc fails but keeps the pointer, which is freed again on the error path.\n\nAn attacker does not need to supply a crafted regular expression: patterns large enough to grow these arrays are common.  The failure has to hit a specific allocation, though, so the issue can only be reached when the system is already under memory pressure.  An attacker who supplies the pattern or the subject string has little control over which allocation fails, so the expected impact is a crash of the process.
